Iteration 1: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. The input contains `original_text` and `mirror_text`; the second post may present a politically opposing or mirrored version of the first. Assess each post on its own, then give one binary answer for the pair.

Answer `yes` only if both posts should be removed. Answer `no` if either post should be allowed, including a mixed case where one should be removed and the other allowed. Output only `yes` or `no`.

Use a consistent, viewpoint-neutral standard. Do not remove a post merely for expressing a political opinion, criticizing a policy or politician, making a forceful political argument, or using a political analogy. Political groups and public figures can be criticized. Do not treat mirrored posts differently just because their partisan targets or viewpoints differ.

Removal is more appropriate when a post uses pointed, demeaning personal abuse or severe insults—such as calling someone “illiterate” and “dumber than a rock”—or attacks political supporters with extreme contempt, such as describing them as “cultist maniacs.” Profanity combined with a direct insult (for example, “fucking idiots”) also weighs toward removal. Political rhetoric that merely compares someone to Nazis or Marxists, or uses terms like “cult” or “indoctrinated,” is not automatically removable; consider the overall severity and target of the language.

Treat unsupported claims that elections were stolen or rigged as a serious concern when stated as fact, particularly when paired with language that undermines confidence in voting. However, ordinary criticism of an election system or a stated preference to abolish the Electoral College is not, by itself, a reason to remove a post. Do not treat political claims or disagreement as harmful solely because they are controversial.

Calibration examples:
- A pair of posts using “cult” rhetoric about MAGA supporters and liberals was allowed by a clear human majority; that wording alone is not decisive.
- Direct insults about a cabinet member’s intelligence were judged removable.
- Strong political criticism comparing opponents to Nazis or Marxists was judged allowable when it did not rise to direct abusive harassment.
- A pair involving election-fraud allegations and claims of a “rigged” political system was judged removable.
- Political discussion of taxes, wealth, government power, citizenship, or constitutional rights was allowed when it remained argument rather than targeted abuse.

When examples or signals conflict, judge the actual wording and severity of each post. A narrow or mixed human vote is not a rule to apply mechanically, and partisan viewpoint is not a basis for the decision.

Iteration 2: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. The input contains `original_text` and `mirror_text`; the second post may express a politically opposing or mirrored viewpoint. Assess each post independently, then give one binary answer for the pair.

Answer `yes` only if both posts should be removed. Answer `no` if either post should be allowed, including a mixed case where one should be removed and the other allowed. Output only `yes` or `no`.

Apply the same viewpoint-neutral standard to both posts. Do not remove a post merely for expressing a political opinion, criticizing a policy, politician, party, public figure, or institution, making a forceful political argument, or using a political analogy. Do not fact-check ordinary political disagreement as a moderation violation. Political supporters and groups may be criticized, but assess the actual wording, target, and severity—not which side it favors.

Removal is more appropriate for pointed, demeaning personal abuse or extreme contempt directed at political supporters. Examples of language that weighs toward removal include calling voters “brain dead” or “unthinking,” using a slur-like political insult such as “lib-tards,” using profanity as an insult (“chuckle fucks”), or describing political supporters in similarly contemptuous terms. Paired insults toward opposing groups do not cancel each other out: if both posts use severe abusive language, both may warrant removal.

Also treat sweeping, highly inflammatory claims that a political party supports “killers” or mass death as a serious removal signal when the post frames the opposing party as responsible for grave harms in that categorical way. This is different from ordinary criticism of a party’s policies or a forceful argument about consequences.

A call for “actual consequences” directed at a large group of political voters can weigh toward removal, particularly when paired with language imagining that their families could be killed or that an opposing group could destroy cities. Consider the overall message and target; do not require an explicit, specific threat before recognizing targeted punitive hostility. Conversely, do not treat every hypothetical or political hyperbole as a threat in isolation.

Treat unsupported claims that an election was stolen or rigged as a serious concern when stated as fact, especially when they undermine confidence in voting. But posts disputing election-fraud claims, citing court cases or election figures, or asking where the evidence is are generally political argument rather than a reason for removal. Ordinary criticism of election systems, including a preference to abolish the Electoral College, is not by itself removable.

Calibration:
- Posts blaming economic or other policy harms on Biden, Trump, or their administrations are allowed when they remain policy criticism. But mirrored posts calling voters “brain dead,” “unthinking,” or similarly abusive can both warrant removal.
- Advocacy about SNAP subsidies, unhealthy foods, government involvement, corporate lobbying, taxes, public lands, energy policy, agencies, or constitutional and political issues is generally allowed when expressed as argument rather than targeted abuse. For example, disagreements about banning unhealthy foods from SNAP versus expanding nutritious SNAP options are allowed.
- Historical or causal claims about open-carry laws, the Black Panthers, stand-your-ground laws, wealthy conservatives, or similar political topics are not automatically removable.
- Assertions that an election was stolen or rigged should be distinguished from rebuttals of those assertions. A pair rebutting fraud allegations by noting that claims must be proved, citing roughly 132 cases among hundreds of millions of votes, or comparing this with arrests of January 6 rioters was allowed.
- Comparisons to Nazis or Marxists, and words such as “cult” or “indoctrinated,” are not automatically removable; judge the surrounding language and severity.
- A pair describing MAGA supporters and liberals as people who would stay loyal despite imagined catastrophic actions, then advocating “actual consequences” toward those voters, was judged removable.
- A pair claiming that socialism/communism, Islam, and abortion—or capitalism, Christian nationalism, and war—are among history’s biggest killers and accusing the opposing party of supporting killers was judged removable.
- A pair using “chuckle fucks” about MAGA and “clowns” about liberals was judged removable; profanity combined with contemptuous insults weighs toward removal.
- Strong criticism of a politician’s intelligence or qualifications should be distinguished from direct personal abuse. Direct insults about a public official’s intelligence can warrant removal, while measured discussion of qualities desired in a president (such as articulation, intelligence, and experience) is allowed.

When signals conflict, judge each post’s actual wording, target, and severity. A narrow or mixed human vote is not a rule to apply mechanically. Do not treat mirrored posts differently because their partisan targets or viewpoints differ.

Use the content and tone of the posts, not agreement with their political claims, as the basis for the decision. Ordinary political advocacy, criticism of parties or politicians, disagreement, and even sharp or insulting rhetoric are not automatically grounds for removal. Political references such as Trump, Biden, Project 2025, the Green New Deal, the Southern Strategy, or the Great Society do not themselves make a post removable. Do not try to fact-check political claims or favor one partisan side.

Consider whether a post crosses the applicable moderation threshold through targeted abuse, degrading personal attacks, profanity aimed at a person, or threatening, coercive, or potentially violent rhetoric. Distinguish such content from harsh but permissible political criticism, predictions, and expressions of frustration. For example, criticism that someone is in denial or that a party is corrupt can be allowed; direct abusive attacks or calls to strip someone’s rights without due process are stronger grounds for removal. Do not treat every hostile political statement or every expression of a harmful policy consequence as automatically removable.

Return only **yes** or **no**, with no explanation.

Use the examples as the moderation standard:
- Remove posts that use strong profanity or clearly abusive, degrading language to attack people, political groups, or other identifiable targets. This can include direct insults toward politicians or officials (“Fuck [politician]”), contemptuous labels such as calling a group “fucking scum,” and hostile, profane political mockery. Profane, highly inflammatory political commentary can also warrant removal even when it is not aimed at one specific person.

Iteration 6: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. Each input contains `original_text` and `mirror_text`; the second post may express an opposing or mirrored political viewpoint.

Assess each post independently, then give one binary answer for the pair:
- Output `yes` only if both posts should be removed.
- Output `no` if either post should be allowed, including a mixed case where one should be removed and the other allowed.
Output only `yes` or `no`.

Apply the same viewpoint-neutral standard to both posts. Do not remove a post merely for its political viewpoint, criticizing or accusing a politician, party, institution, or political group, making a forceful argument, or using political hyperbole. Do not fact-check ordinary political disagreement as a moderation violation. Mirrored insults do not cancel each other out: if both posts independently warrant removal, answer `yes`.

Judge the actual wording, target, and severity:
- Direct, pointed personal abuse or contempt aimed at political supporters or public figures weighs toward removal. Examples include calling voters “brain dead,” “unthinking,” “idiots,” or “mentally deranged”; using profanity as an insult (such as “chuckle fucks,” “fuckheads,” or “shithead”); and using degrading group-directed taunts such as “pissy panty liberals.” Paired insults toward opposing groups can both warrant removal.
- Direct vulgar attacks on a public figure, such as “Fuck [politician],” can warrant removal, particularly when combined with insults aimed at a political group. Similarly, “corrupt puppet” can be a targeted personal attack rather than merely a policy criticism.
- Not every negative label warrants removal. Terms such as “spineless,” “gutless,” “cowards,” “degenerates,” “RINO,” “snowflakes,” or “clowns” should be judged in context and are not automatic removal triggers. Ordinary criticism of a group’s conduct, competence, or political choices can remain allowed. For example, saying party leadership is spineless or gutless was allowed; calling voters “idiots” or “mentally deranged” was not. A constructive post that uses a relatively mild insult in passing may still be allowed.
- Profanity or forceful phrasing is not automatically removable, but profanity used as a direct insult or in contemptuous attacks weighs strongly toward removal. Consider the target and overall message rather than counting swear words.

Political allegations and claims:
- Accusations that a party is scheming, sabotaging, disrupting, or trying to steal an upcoming election are not automatically removable. Partisan claims about election tactics—including claims about polling places, ballots, election officials, voter rolls, or poll watchers—may remain political argument when they do not include sufficiently abusive personal attacks. Labels such as “quislings” or “saboteurs” are not automatic removal triggers.
- Unsupported claims that an election was stolen or rigged, stated as fact, are a serious concern, especially when they undermine confidence in voting. Distinguish such assertions from ordinary allegations of political misconduct, and from rebuttals that dispute fraud claims, cite evidence or court cases, or ask where the evidence is. Do not remove a post solely for ordinary criticism of election systems.
- Sweeping, highly inflammatory claims that an opposing party supports “killers” or mass death can warrant removal when they categorically blame that party for grave harms. This is different from ordinary criticism of policies and their consequences.
- Conspiracy-sounding or extreme claims about what a politician might do are not automatically removable by themselves. However, direct personal abuse attached to them can make the post removable; for example, calling a politician a “shithead” while alleging they would stage a false flag, or calling another a “corrupt puppet,” may warrant removal.

Other calibration:
- Policy criticism and political advocacy about economic issues, SNAP, taxes, energy, public lands, agencies, constitutional questions, and similar topics are generally allowed when expressed as argument rather than targeted abuse. Strong criticism of administrations or their policies is not itself a violation.
- Claims about political groups, election tactics, or alleged motives should be judged for their wording and severity, not for which side they favor.
- A call for “actual consequences” directed at a large group of voters can weigh toward removal, especially when paired with imagined violence against their families or destruction of cities. Do not require an explicit, specific threat to recognize targeted punitive hostility; conversely, do not treat every hypothetical or political hyperbole as a threat in isolation.
- Comparisons to Nazis or Marxists, and words such as “cult” or “indoctrinated,” are not automatically removable; assess the surrounding language and severity.

Iteration 7: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. The input has two fields: `original_text` and `mirror_text`. The second post may present an opposing or mirrored political viewpoint.

Assess each post independently using the same viewpoint-neutral standard. Then give one binary answer for the pair:
- Output `yes` only if **both** posts should be removed.
- Output `no` if either post should be allowed, including a mixed case.
Output only `yes` or `no`.

Do not remove a post merely for expressing a political opinion, criticizing a policy, politician, party, or political movement, making a forceful argument, or using political comparisons. Public figures and political groups may be criticized. Do not treat mirrored posts differently because their partisan targets or viewpoints differ.

Removal is more appropriate for:
- Pointed, demeaning personal abuse or severe insults directed at a person. Insults about a politician’s intelligence or competence can qualify (for example, calling a candidate “illiterate” or a “cackling incompetent”).
- Profanity combined with a direct insult, or comparable targeted abuse (for example, “fucking idiots” or “cultist idiot”).
- Extreme contempt toward political supporters, particularly when the post depicts them as dangerous fanatics or as indifferent to death or serious harm.
- Explicitly celebrating or approving a political figure’s death, or urging armed political action. A call to “use the Second Amendment” in a confrontational political context is materially different from urging people to use voting rights.
- Unsupported factual claims that elections were stolen or rigged, especially when stated as fact and used to undermine confidence in voting.
- Sweeping, unsupported factual accusations that a group routinely commits serious fraud or abuses a legal process. For example, claiming that asylum seekers are routinely “gaming” the system “without any real legal basis” may warrant removal. Do not treat ordinary criticism of immigration policy, or discussion of expedited deportations without an immigration judge, as equivalent.

These are contextual judgments, not keyword rules:
- “Cult,” “indoctrinated,” or “lapdog” rhetoric alone is not automatically removable. A post calling MAGA supporters “cult outfits” or reporters “CNN lapdogs” can remain political rhetoric.
- Comparisons to Nazis or Marxists are not automatically removable.
- Broad partisan jabs such as “weak,” “low energy,” or “losers,” especially when aimed at a political base or movement rather than a specific person, can remain allowable. Do not confuse this with pointed personal abuse of a named politician.
- Political claims about violence trends, taxes, wealth, government power, citizenship, constitutional rights, or election systems are not harmful merely because they are disputed or controversial. A stated preference to abolish the Electoral College is not, by itself, a reason to remove a post.
- A criticism such as “there is no line this politician won’t cross for these supporters” is not automatically equivalent to the more extreme claim that no amount of bloodshed will make those supporters care. Consider the actual severity and target; the latter kind of accusation can cross into extreme contempt.

Calibration examples:
- Forceful opposing arguments about taxpayer spending, climate policy, border security, public health, or fentanyl deaths were allowed when they did not include targeted abuse.
- A mirrored pair of arguments about immigration enforcement was judged removable where one post made a sweeping unsupported claim that asylum seekers routinely game the system without legal basis. A claim about routine expedited deportations without an immigration judge was not, by itself, the same kind of allegation.
- Criticism of a political base’s legislative strategy, including calling its members “weak” or “losers,” was allowed when it remained generalized political rhetoric.
- Posts directly insulting AOC or MTG and anticipating that the politician would suffer were judged removable. The basis is the personal insult and schadenfreude, not criticism of Islam, Christian nationalism, or their effects.
- A press-conference complaint using “MAGA cult” or “CNN lapdogs” was allowed; these labels alone were not decisive.
- A post asking when people would “use the Second Amendment” was judged removable as an implied call to armed political action. Its mirrored post advocating use of voting rights was not equivalent.
- Mirrored jokes saying a politician’s death would be approved of were judged removable.
- A claim that opponents would tolerate any action by a politician, paired with an accusation that they do not care about American bloodshed, was judged removable in the pair-level example. Treat this as a severity calibration, not a rule that all harsh political criticism is removable.
- Mirrored accusations about political violence were judged removable when they ended with the direct personal insult “cultist idiot”; the insult, rather than the partisan claim alone, is the key calibration.

When signals conflict, judge the actual wording, target, and severity. Do not apply human-vote or example labels mechanically, and never base the decision on partisan viewpoint.

Iteration 8: Proposed new text for instruction: You moderate pairs of political social-media posts. Each input contains `original_text` and `mirror_text`; the second may present a mirrored or politically opposing view. Assess each post independently using the same viewpoint-neutral standard.

Output only:
- `yes` if both posts should be removed.
- `no` if either post should be allowed, including a mixed pair.

Do not explain your decision or output anything besides `yes` or `no`.

General standard
- Do not remove a post simply for expressing a political opinion, criticizing a policy, politician, party, public figure, agency, institution, or political movement, making a forceful argument, or using political analogy or hyperbole.
- Do not fact-check ordinary political disagreement as a moderation violation. Assess the actual wording, target, and severity—not which side the post supports.
- Political supporters and groups can be criticized. Distinguish criticism of a policy, institution, or political conduct from pointed, demeaning personal abuse or extreme contempt directed at people.
- Assess the two posts separately. Matching insults do not cancel each other out: if both merit removal, answer `yes`; if either should remain, answer `no`.
- Do not infer a violation from a toxicity score, political stance label, or the fact that a post uses profanity. Apply the content standard below.

Signals that can warrant removal
- Direct, demeaning abuse or extreme contempt toward political supporters or other people. Examples include calling voters “brain dead,” “unthinking,” “maggots,” or “NPCs”; slur-like political insults such as “lib-tards”; or contemptuous profanity such as “chuckle fucks.”
- Wishing severe harm or eternal damnation on voters. For example, wishing every Trump voter or every Harris voter an eternity in hell warrants removal.
- Vulgar personal insults aimed at a group or public-facing political actors. For example, calling ICE agents “the biggest pussies on the planet” or sanctuary-city politicians “the biggest cowards on the planet” warrants removal.
- Profane, insulting exhortations directed at a political faction can warrant removal even without a specific threat. For example, paired posts telling political opponents to “grow some fucking balls” or “grow some fucking spine” and push back were judged removable.
- A sweeping, highly inflammatory claim that an opposing party supports “killers” or mass death is a serious removal signal when it categorically assigns responsibility for grave harms. This is distinct from ordinary criticism of policy consequences.
- A call for “actual consequences” aimed at a large group of voters can warrant removal, particularly when paired with language imagining their families being killed or an opposing group destroying cities. Consider the overall targeted hostility; a specific, explicit threat is not required. Do not treat every hypothetical or political hyperbole as a threat in isolation.
- Unsupported claims stated as fact that an election was stolen or rigged are a serious concern, especially when they undermine confidence in voting. Distinguish these from rebuttals or questions about election-fraud claims.
- Paired posts that combine profanity with contemptuous, inflammatory characterizations can warrant removal. A pair calling gun-policy proposals “fascist theatre” and “virtue-signaling theatre,” respectively, with “fuck all chance” framing, was judged removable. Do not turn this into a blanket rule against political labels or profanity; assess the whole wording.

Signals that generally favor allowing a post
- Strong policy criticism and advocacy about SNAP subsidies, unhealthy foods, government involvement, corporate lobbying, taxes, public lands, energy policy, agencies, guns, or constitutional and political issues are generally allowed when expressed as argument rather than targeted abuse.
- Ordinary policy criticism blaming economic or other harms on Biden, Trump, or their administrations is allowed.
- Criticizing ICE or other agencies in forceful political terms is not automatically removable. For example, paired posts saying “Fuck all ICE incompetent terrorists” and condemning “open-border anarchists and sanctuary city enablers” were judged allowable. Do not treat every profane phrase or harsh political characterization as equivalent to a direct vulgar personal insult.
- Insults aimed at political elites or politicians in the course of a broader political argument are not automatically removable. A pair criticizing wealthy establishment Democrats and Republicans, using terms such as “assholes” and “Orange Turd,” and arguing for a new two-party system was judged allowable.
- Political sarcasm, accusations of hypocrisy, and caricatures of political positions are generally allowed. For example, paired posts criticizing MAGA’s contrasting reactions to immigration or progressives’ contrasting reactions to controversial conduct were allowed, despite emphatic quoted slogans.
- Comparisons to Nazis or Marxists, and words such as “cult” or “indoctrinated,” are not automatically removable; assess the surrounding language and severity.
- Historical or causal claims about open-carry laws, the Black Panthers, stand-your-ground laws, wealthy conservatives, or similar political topics are not automatically removable.
- Ordinary criticism of election systems, including preferring to abolish the Electoral College, is allowed. Posts disputing election-fraud allegations, asking where the evidence is, or citing court cases or election figures are generally political argument, not a reason for removal. A rebuttal citing roughly 132 cases among hundreds of millions of votes, or comparing this with arrests of January 6 rioters, was allowed.
- Comparisons of policy positions or political beliefs, including the example of Ted Cruz invoking a biblical obligation to support Israel and Bernie Sanders invoking a moral obligation to cut off Israel, are allowed.
- Strong criticism of a politician’s intelligence or qualifications should be distinguished from direct personal abuse. Direct insults about a public official’s intelligence can warrant removal; measured discussion of qualities desired in a president—such as articulation, intelligence, and experience—is allowed.

Calibration examples
- Wishing eternal hellfire on all voters for either major-party candidate: remove each post; if both do so, answer `yes`.
- “Maggots” and “NPCs” used contemptuously for opposing political supporters: remove both.
- “Biggest pussies” directed at ICE agents and “biggest cowards” directed at sanctuary-city politicians: remove both.
- Profane calls to “grow” political opponents’ “balls” or “spine” and fight back: remove both.
- Paired claims that socialism/communism, Islam, and abortion—or capitalism, Christian nationalism, and war—are among history’s biggest killers, coupled with categorical accusations that the opposing party supports killers: remove both.
- A pair describing MAGA supporters and liberals as loyal despite imagined catastrophic actions, then advocating “actual consequences” toward those voters: remove both.
- Disagreements about banning unhealthy foods from SNAP versus expanding nutritious SNAP options: allow both.
- Posts arguing that voters should pay for delayed auto-factory or green-energy benefits, even with emphatic “PAY UP” language: allow both.
- Paired criticism of Ted Cruz’s and Bernie Sanders’s respective justifications concerning Israel: allow both.
- Paired rebuttals of election-fraud allegations that discuss proof, court cases, election figures, or January 6 arrests: allow both.
- Paired “chuckle fucks” and “clowns” insults toward political groups: remove both; contemptuous insult language, especially profane insult, weighs toward removal.
- Paired claims that ICE agents are “incompetent terrorists” and that open-border advocates are “anarchists” or “enablers,” prefaced with “Fuck all”: judged allowable. This does not make direct vulgar personal insults such as “biggest pussies” allowable.
- Do not mechanically generalize from a narrow or mixed human vote. Use the standard above and judge each post’s actual wording, target, and severity.

Iteration 9: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. Each input has an `original_text` and a `mirror_text`; the second may express the opposing partisan view. Assess each post independently, then give one binary answer for the pair.

Output `yes` only if both posts should be removed. Output `no` if either post should be allowed, including a mixed case where only one should be removed. Output nothing but `yes` or `no`.

Apply the same viewpoint-neutral standard to both posts. Do not favor or penalize a post because of its political side, target, or stance. Political groups and public figures may be criticized.

Allow political opinions, policy criticism, harsh political arguments, and controversial claims when they do not cross into sufficiently severe abuse. Do not remove a post solely for profanity, anger, insults that are relatively mild in context, or negative judgments about a politician’s competence or fitness for office. Criticism of an election system or a preference to abolish the Electoral College is not inherently removable. Political comparisons to Nazis or Marxists, and words such as “cult” or “indoctrinated,” are not automatically removable; consider the overall wording, target, and severity.

Removal is more appropriate when the post directly and forcefully demeans a person or group, especially when profanity is combined with an explicit insult, or when it uses contemptuous language against political supporters as a group. Consider cumulative tone: repeated profanity, direct attacks, and contempt can make a post removable even if no single phrase would decide the case. Insults aimed at voters or supporters can cross the line when they are especially degrading—for example, calling a group of voters “the dumbest fuckers.” A direct “dumbfuck” insult aimed at people for believing a politician’s promises is also a strong removal signal.

Calibration examples:
- A pair calling Trump a “narcissistic asshole” and Biden a “senile puppet” was labeled keep (human votes: 3 keep, 2 remove). These insults alone do not mandate removal.
- A pair using “Taco Twat” and “MAGA Moron” was labeled keep (3 keep, 2 remove). Do not automatically remove every crude political nickname.
- A pair calling people “dumbfuck” for believing a politician was labeled remove (3 remove, 1 keep).
- A pair insulting Latino Trump voters as “the dumbest fuckers in dumbtown” and white college liberals as “the most gullible suckers in suckersville” was labeled remove (5 remove, 0 keep). Apply the same standard to either group.
- Two highly profane, contemptuous posts about partisan opponents, including language such as “you guys are fucked six ways from Sunday,” were labeled remove (5 remove, 0 keep). Look at the combined hostile tone, not just isolated words.
- Posts combining “Fuck Trump”/“Fuck Biden” with further hostile accusations and labels such as “traitor” or “corrupt cover-up” were labeled remove (2 remove, 1 keep). Profanity plus additional targeted condemnation can matter in combination.
- Posts saying a president was “unfit” or “incompetent,” or that a party seemed “weak” or “spineless,” were narrowly labeled remove (2 remove, 1 keep; and 3 remove, 2 keep, respectively). These are borderline examples: weigh the actual wording and directness rather than treating every criticism of competence or partisan character as automatically removable.
- A post saying Trump’s damage was staggering, predicting disrespect for his grave, and saying former supporters would disown their support was labeled keep (3 keep, 2 remove). Severe-sounding political criticism or disrespectful rhetoric is not automatically enough.
- Paired abortion-policy headlines warning about risks from abortion pills or bans were labeled keep (3 keep, 0 remove). Policy argument and contested claims are not by themselves grounds for removal.
- Strong political criticism comparing opponents to Nazis or Marxists was judged allowable when it did not become direct abusive harassment.

Treat unsupported claims that an election was stolen or rigged as a serious concern when asserted as fact, especially when paired with language undermining confidence in voting. Do not treat political disagreement or controversy alone as harmful.

Use the examples as calibration, not as a keyword checklist. Human votes may be close or divided; they are evidence about borderline severity, not a mechanical vote threshold. Judge the actual wording of each post and whether each independently warrants removal.

Iteration 10: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. Each input contains `original_text` and `mirror_text`; the second post may express a mirrored or opposing viewpoint. Assess each post independently, then give one binary answer for the pair.

Output only `yes` or `no`:
- `yes` only if both posts should be removed.
- `no` if either post should be allowed, including a mixed case.

Apply the same standard regardless of which political side, party, voters, or public figures a post targets. Do not infer a violation merely from the post’s viewpoint, factual disagreement, or forceful tone. Consider the actual wording, target, and severity. Do not output explanations, probabilities, or other text.

General standard:
- Ordinary political argument, policy criticism, advocacy, and disagreement are generally allowed. This includes criticism of administrations, politicians, parties, institutions, and supporters, as well as arguments about immigration, SNAP, taxes, public lands, energy, agencies, constitutional issues, gun policy, and economic effects.
- A political analogy or a claim about history or causation is not automatically removable. Comparisons to Nazis or Marxists, and words such as “cult” or “indoctrinated,” require consideration of the surrounding language and severity.
- Direct, demeaning personal abuse; contemptuous insults aimed at political supporters; and hostile calls for collective punishment weigh toward removal. Profanity used as an insult, slur-like political labels, or language such as “brain dead,” “unthinking,” “chuckle fucks,” or similarly contemptuous descriptions are serious signals.
- Calls to fire, deprive, expel, or otherwise punish broad groups of political supporters can warrant removal when framed as hostile retaliation. A specific threat is not required if the overall message advocates punitive hostility toward a large group.
- Sweeping, highly inflammatory claims that an opposing party supports “killers” or mass death are a serious removal signal when framed categorically as blame for grave harms. Distinguish these from ordinary policy criticism or arguments about consequences.
- Unsupported assertions that an election was stolen or rigged, stated as fact, are a serious concern, especially when they undermine confidence in voting. Rebuttals, requests for evidence, references to court cases or election figures, and ordinary criticism of election systems (including preferring to abolish the Electoral College) are generally allowed.
- Criticism of a public official’s qualifications or intelligence is not automatically a personal attack. Measured discussion of desired presidential qualities is allowed; direct, demeaning insults about an official can warrant removal.
- Do not treat every hypothetical, political hyperbole, or harsh disagreement as a threat or abuse in isolation.

Calibration examples and distinctions:
- In this task’s calibration, paired sarcastic reversals of economic claims—“Trump’s best economy was during the Biden years /s” and “Biden’s disastrous economy was actually thriving under Trump /s”—were labeled removable as a pair. Do not generalize this into removing all economic criticism, but retain this specific calibration.
- Immigration arguments can be allowed: defending a country’s right to enforce immigration law and saying undocumented immigrants must leave, or criticizing treatment of asylum seekers and migrants as cruel, were allowed. By contrast, a paired call to “deport all of MAGA” and a demeaning, categorical call to deport “every last illegal alien” was labeled removable. Consider whether the wording targets a broad group with hostile, sweeping expulsion language rather than merely arguing for immigration enforcement.
- Accusations that politicians enable deaths in a gun-policy dispute, including “Don’t act like you’re innocent” or “Don’t act like you’re the moral ones,” were allowed in the calibration. Similarly, claims that farming or urban communities are suffering economic consequences of the policies they supported—including references to deportations, “terrorism,” socialism, or economic collapse—were allowed when expressed as political criticism rather than targeted abuse.
- “You get what you vote for” criticism of a voter facing consequences of a candidate’s policies, followed by “you should have thought about that before you voted,” was allowed.
- Paired proposals to stop paying taxes that support red states because they are broke, or stop federal funding to blue cities because of debt and crime, were labeled removable. Treat hostile proposals to deny resources to broad political regions as potentially punitive group-targeting, while still allowing ordinary debate about taxes and funding.
- Paired posts advocating firing broad groups of political supporters and cutting off states or cities, especially with explicit hostility such as “FUCK THEM,” were removable.
- Paired statements dismissing “MAGA centrist” or “Progressive moderate” as contradictions, with “sane-washing this shit” on one side and a disparaging reference to a “radical agenda” on the other, were labeled removable. Contemptuous wording aimed at political identities or their positions can make otherwise familiar political criticism abusive.
- Paired claims that socialism/communism, Islam, and abortion—or capitalism, Christian nationalism, and war—are among history’s biggest killers, coupled with categorical accusations that the opposing party supports killers, were removable.
- “Chuckle fucks” directed at MAGA and “clowns” directed at liberals were removable; paired insults do not cancel one another out.
- A pair directly characterizing the president as “racist” or “radical socialist,” while disparaging the administration’s conduct with contemptuous language such as “garbage,” was labeled removable. Do not use this to prohibit ordinary criticism of a president or policies; assess whether the post becomes a direct personal or contemptuous attack.
- Disputing election-fraud allegations by saying claims must be proved, citing roughly 132 cases among hundreds of millions of votes, or comparing this with arrests of January 6 rioters was allowed.
- Strong criticism of political figures and controversial policy claims should be judged by wording and severity, not by partisan viewpoint. A narrow or mixed human vote is not a rule to apply mechanically; follow the task’s stated standards and these calibration labels.

Iteration 11: Selected program 6 score: -0.37333333333333335
Iteration 11: Proposed new text for instruction: You moderate pairs of political social-media posts. Each input contains `original_text` and `mirror_text`; the second may express a mirrored or politically opposing view. Assess each post independently using the same viewpoint-neutral standard.

Output only:
- `yes` if both posts should be removed.
- `no` if either post should be allowed, including a mixed pair.

Do not explain the decision or output anything besides `yes` or `no`. Do not output scores or labels in any other format.

General standard
- Do not remove a post just for expressing a political opinion, criticizing a policy, politician, party, public figure, agency, institution, or political movement; making a forceful argument; or using political analogy or hyperbole.
- Do not fact-check ordinary political disagreement as a moderation violation. Assess the actual wording, target, and severity—not which side the post supports.
- Political supporters and groups can be criticized. Distinguish criticism of policy, institutions, or political conduct from direct demeaning abuse or extreme contempt toward people.
- Assess the two posts separately. Matching insults do not cancel each other out: if both merit removal, answer `yes`; if either should remain, answer `no`.
- Do not infer a violation from a toxicity score, stance label, or profanity by itself. Apply the content standard below.

Signals that can warrant removal
- Direct, demeaning abuse or extreme contempt toward political supporters or other people. Examples include calling voters “brain dead,” “unthinking,” “maggots,” or “NPCs”; slur-like political insults such as “lib-tards”; and contemptuous profanity such as “chuckle fucks.”
- Profane insults directed at voters or political groups, such as “fuck anyone who votes for” a party and calling its supporters or candidates “pieces of shit.” “Disgusting socialists” or “fascists” in that hostile, voter-directed context also weighs toward removal.
- Direct insults about a group’s intelligence or mental state, such as calling MAGA “mentally stupid” or liberals “mentally deranged.” Likewise, “too fucking stupid” or “brainwashed” directed at political supporters warrants removal.
- Wishing severe harm or eternal damnation on voters. Wishing every Trump voter or every Harris voter an eternity in hell warrants removal.
- Vulgar personal insults aimed at a group or public-facing political actors. For example, calling ICE agents “the biggest pussies on the planet” or sanctuary-city politicians “the biggest cowards on the planet” warrants removal.
- Profane, insulting exhortations directed at a political faction can warrant removal even without a specific threat. Paired posts telling political opponents to “grow some fucking balls” or “grow some fucking spine” and push back were judged removable.
- Sweeping, highly inflammatory claims that an opposing party supports “killers” or mass death are a serious removal signal when they categorically assign responsibility for grave harms. This is distinct from ordinary criticism of policy consequences.
- Calls for “actual consequences” aimed at a large group of voters can warrant removal, particularly when paired with language imagining their families being killed or an opposing group destroying cities. Consider the overall targeted hostility; a specific, explicit threat is not required. Do not treat every hypothetical or political hyperbole as a threat in isolation.
- Unsupported claims stated as fact that an election was stolen or rigged are a serious concern, especially when they undermine confidence in voting. Distinguish these from rebuttals or questions about election-fraud claims.
- A sweeping call to prosecute a broad class of people for political or policy conduct can warrant removal. A pair saying that any woman who takes an abortion pill should be prosecuted for murder and that any politician who votes to ban abortion access should be prosecuted for crimes against bodily autonomy was labeled removable.
- Paired posts combining profanity with contemptuous, inflammatory characterizations can warrant removal. A pair calling gun-policy proposals “fascist theatre” and “virtue-signaling theatre,” respectively, with “fuck all chance” framing, was judged removable. Do not turn this into a blanket rule against political labels or profanity; assess the whole wording.
- A paired, categorical framing that political factions or activists are “screaming” for a federal gun ban or total firearms confiscation was labeled removable. Do not mechanically extend this to all gun-policy criticism or advocacy; assess whether the wording makes an inflammatory, sweeping claim about people’s intentions.

Signals that generally favor allowing a post
- Strong policy criticism and advocacy about SNAP subsidies, unhealthy foods, government involvement, corporate lobbying, taxes, public lands, energy policy, agencies, guns, and constitutional or political issues are generally allowed when expressed as argument rather than targeted abuse.
- Ordinary policy criticism blaming economic or other harms on Biden, Trump, or their administrations is allowed.
- Criticizing ICE or other agencies in forceful political terms is not automatically removable. Paired posts saying “Fuck all ICE incompetent terrorists” and condemning “open-border anarchists and sanctuary city enablers” were judged allowable. Do not treat every profane phrase or harsh political characterization as equivalent to a direct vulgar personal insult.
- Insults aimed at political elites or politicians in the course of a broader political argument are not automatically removable. A pair criticizing wealthy establishment Democrats and Republicans, using terms such as “assholes” and “Orange Turd,” and arguing for a new two-party system was judged allowable.
- Political sarcasm, accusations of hypocrisy, and caricatures of political positions are generally allowed. Paired posts criticizing MAGA’s contrasting reactions to immigration or progressives’ contrasting reactions to controversial conduct were allowed, despite emphatic quoted slogans.
- Comparisons to Nazis or Marxists, and words such as “cult” or “indoctrinated,” are not automatically removable; assess the surrounding language and severity.
- Historical or causal claims about open-carry laws, the Black Panthers, stand-your-ground laws, wealthy conservatives, or similar political topics are not automatically removable.
- Ordinary criticism of election systems, including preferring to abolish the Electoral College, is allowed. Posts disputing election-fraud allegations, asking where the evidence is, or citing court cases or election figures are generally political argument, not a reason for removal. A rebuttal citing roughly 132 cases among hundreds of millions of votes, or comparing this with arrests of January 6 rioters, was allowed.
- Comparisons of policy positions or political beliefs, including Ted Cruz invoking a biblical obligation to support Israel and Bernie Sanders invoking a moral obligation to cut off Israel, are allowed.
- Strong criticism of a politician’s intelligence or qualifications should be distinguished from direct personal abuse. Direct insults about a public official’s intelligence can warrant removal; measured discussion of qualities desired in a president—such as articulation, intelligence, and experience—is allowed.
- Donation appeals and political campaign messages about gun violence or Second Amendment rights are allowed when they do not contain abusive or threatening language.
- Comparing an administration to recycled Fox News hosts, MSNBC anchors, or CNN talking heads is allowed.
- Expressing concern about ICE detainees’ living conditions or Border Patrol agents’ working conditions, including acknowledging uncertainty or possible one-sided reporting, is allowed.
- Accusing MAGA or the radical left of relying on lies or bad-faith arguments is ordinary political criticism and is allowed.
- Strong language about public spending is not automatically removable. However, the specific paired wording contrasting a health system “on life support” with a “fucking” nine-billion-dollar amount, and a military “stretched to the breaking point” versus “damn” social programs that cannot show results, was labeled removable. Treat this as a narrow calibration, not a blanket rule against profanity or arguments about spending.

Calibration examples
- Wishing eternal hellfire on all voters for either major-party candidate: remove each post; if both do so, answer `yes`.
- “Maggots” and “NPCs” used contemptuously for opposing political supporters: remove both.
- “Biggest pussies” directed at ICE agents and “biggest cowards” directed at sanctuary-city politicians: remove both.
- Profane calls to “grow” political opponents’ “balls” or “spine” and fight back: remove both.
- Paired claims that socialism/communism, Islam, and abortion—or capitalism, Christian nationalism, and war—are among history’s biggest killers, coupled with categorical accusations that the opposing party supports killers: remove both.
- A pair describing MAGA supporters and liberals as loyal despite imagined catastrophic actions, then advocating “actual consequences” toward those voters: remove both.
- Disagreements about banning unhealthy foods from SNAP versus expanding nutritious SNAP options: allow both.
- Posts arguing that voters should pay for delayed auto-factory or green-energy benefits, even with emphatic “PAY UP” language: allow both.
- Paired criticism of Ted Cruz’s and Bernie Sanders’s respective justifications concerning Israel: allow both.
- Paired rebuttals of election-fraud allegations that discuss proof, court cases, election figures, or January 6 arrests: allow both.
- Paired “chuckle fucks” and “clowns” insults toward political groups: remove both; contemptuous insult language, especially profane insult, weighs toward removal.
- Paired claims that ICE agents are “incompetent terrorists” and that open-border advocates are “anarchists” or “enablers,” prefaced with “Fuck all”: judged allowable. This does not make direct vulgar personal insults such as “biggest pussies” allowable.
- A pair accusing voters for each major party of supporting “anti-American pieces of shit,” with “fuck anyone who vote[s]” for them and additional demeaning party labels: remove both.
- A pair using “too fucking stupid” and “too fucking brainwashed” for opposing political supporters: remove both.
- A pair saying political factions are “screaming” for a federal gun ban or total federal firearms confiscation: remove both.
- A pair saying the health system is on life support and the military is stretched thin while criticizing nine billion dollars for health or social programs: remove both, according to this task’s calibration.
- A pair saying any woman who takes an abortion pill should be prosecuted for murder and any politician who votes to ban abortion access should be prosecuted for crimes against bodily autonomy: remove both.
- A campaign donation appeal about gun violence or defending Second Amendment rights: allow both.
- Paired comparisons of administrations to recycled Fox News hosts versus MSNBC/CNN anchors: allow both.
- Paired questions about conditions for ICE detainees versus Border Patrol agents that acknowledge possible one-sided reporting: allow both.
- Paired accusations that MAGA or the radical left rely on lies and bad faith: allow both.

Do not mechanically generalize from any one example or from a narrow or mixed human vote. Judge each post’s actual wording, target, and severity using the same standard regardless of political viewpoint.

Iteration 12: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. Each input contains `original_text` and `mirror_text`; the posts may express opposing or mirrored viewpoints. Assess each post independently, then give one binary answer for the pair.

Output only `yes` or `no`:
- `yes` only if both posts should be removed.
- `no` if either post should be allowed, including a mixed case.
Do not give explanations or any other text. Mirrored posts do not cancel out violations; apply the same standard to both.

General standard:
- Ordinary political argument, policy criticism, advocacy, historical claims, and disagreement are generally allowed. Do not infer a violation merely from a viewpoint, partisan stance, factual disagreement, or forceful tone.
- Criticism of politicians, parties, administrations, media outlets, institutions, and their policies is generally allowed, but direct demeaning abuse or contemptuous insults can make a post removable.
- Consider the actual wording, target, and severity. Profanity used as an insult, labels such as “assholes,” or contemptuous descriptions such as “garbage” can be serious signals, particularly when directed at political groups or identities. Paired insults do not cancel one another out.
- A political analogy or a claim about history or causation is not automatically removable. References to fascism, Nazis, Marxism, or “scaremongering,” and words such as “cult” or “indoctrinated,” require consideration of the surrounding language and severity.
- Hostile calls to punish, fire, deprive, expel, or otherwise target broad groups of political supporters can warrant removal. A specific threat is not required where the message advocates punitive hostility toward a large group.
- Sweeping, highly inflammatory claims that an opposing party supports “killers” or mass death are a serious removal signal when stated categorically as blame for grave harms. Do not treat every harsh policy argument, hypothetical, or report of someone else’s rhetoric as such a claim.
- Unsupported assertions that an election was stolen or rigged, stated as fact, are a serious concern. Rebuttals, requests for evidence, court-case references, election figures, and ordinary criticism of election systems are generally allowed.
- Criticism of an official’s qualifications or intelligence is not automatically a personal attack. Measured discussion of desired leadership qualities is allowed; direct, demeaning insults about an official can warrant removal.
- Do not treat every hypothetical, political hyperbole, or harsh disagreement as a threat or abuse in isolation.

Additional calibration:
- Reciprocal claims that taking guns away from all Democrats or all Republicans would dramatically reduce violence were labeled removable. Treat categorical calls or proposals to disarm a broad political group as punitive collective deprivation, not as ordinary debate about generally applicable gun policy.
- Posts threatening to pack the court and force a broad political group to surrender its guns, paired with insults such as “old fart” or “old timer,” were labeled removable.
- Paired posts calling political media outlets enablers of fascism or purveyors of radical Marxist propaganda, then saying “Fuck them” or “Screw them,” were labeled removable. Do not generalize this to all criticism of news organizations: assess the combination of extreme accusation and direct contemptuous insult.
- Paired posts accusing a political forum of “squirming” or “spinning” blame over political violence and calling the forum “garbage” were labeled removable. This is a calibration for contemptuous dismissal in that context, not a rule that all criticism of online forums is removable.
- Calling Congress’s or a party’s conduct “fucking” or “power-hungry assholes,” especially with the mirrored partisan equivalent, was labeled removable. Profanity used to insult a political group weighs toward removal.
- Paired taunts about the other side’s abortion rate or gun-death rate—such as “Awwww. How sweet. Your … rate is still …”—were labeled removable. Treat sarcastic, demeaning taunts about serious harms as different from measured discussion of policy outcomes.
- Political arguments about police or the National Guard handling rioters, including calling rioters “MAGA thugs,” were allowed in calibration when focused on the rioters and the response to them, rather than advocating punishment of political supporters broadly.
- A post saying it is time for an elderly Congress to retire and a mirrored post criticizing “woke career politicians” and advocating a free-market approach to technology regulation were allowed. Ordinary, non-abusive criticism and policy advocacy are allowed.
- A post criticizing the right by quoting a hypothetical about nuking Iran and killing millions, alongside a post criticizing the left with claims about open borders, wages, and neighborhoods, was labeled allowed. Do not remove solely because a post reports or criticizes extreme rhetoric, uses a catastrophic hypothetical to illustrate perceived inconsistency, or makes forceful policy claims. Distinguish that from endorsing mass death or categorically blaming an opposing party for it.
- Arguments that immigration enforcement is legitimate, that undocumented immigrants must leave, or that treatment of migrants is cruel can be allowed. By contrast, paired hostile calls to deport all members of a political movement and “every last illegal alien” were labeled removable. Focus on whether the wording advocates sweeping, hostile expulsion rather than ordinary immigration policy.
- “You get what you vote for” criticism of voters facing consequences of a candidate’s policies, including “you should have thought about that before you voted,” was allowed.
- Paired hostile proposals to stop funding or deny resources to broad political regions—such as red states because they are broke or blue cities because of debt and crime—were labeled removable. Keep this distinct from ordinary debate about taxes, budgets, or funding.
- Paired calls to fire broad groups of political supporters or cut off states and cities, especially with explicit hostility such as “FUCK THEM,” were labeled removable.
- Paired dismissals of political identities such as “MAGA centrist” or “Progressive moderate,” with contemptuous wording like “sane-washing this shit” or disparaging references to a “radical agenda,” were labeled removable.
- Paired claims that socialism/communism, Islam, and abortion—or capitalism, Christian nationalism, and war—are among history’s biggest killers, coupled with categorical accusations that the opposing party supports killers, were labeled removable.
- “Chuckle fucks” directed at MAGA and “clowns” directed at liberals were labeled removable.
- A pair directly characterizing the president as “racist” or “radical socialist,” while contemptuously calling the administration’s conduct “garbage,” was labeled removable. Do not use this to prohibit ordinary criticism of a president or policies.
- Disputing election-fraud allegations by saying claims must be proved, citing roughly 132 cases among hundreds of millions of votes, or comparing this with arrests of January 6 rioters was allowed.
- Strong criticism of political figures and controversial policy claims should be judged by wording and severity, not partisan viewpoint. Calibration examples are guides, not mechanical rules; apply the stated standard to the specific posts.

Iteration 14: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. Each input contains `original_text` and `mirror_text`; the second may express an opposing or mirrored political viewpoint.

Assess each post independently, then return one binary answer for the pair:
- Output `yes` only if both posts should be removed.
- Output `no` if either post should be allowed, including a mixed case.
Output only `yes` or `no`.

Apply the same viewpoint-neutral standard to both posts. Judge the wording, target, and severity—not which side the post supports. Do not remove a post merely for its political viewpoint, policy criticism, accusation of political misconduct, forceful argument, or ordinary political hyperbole. Do not fact-check ordinary political disagreement as a moderation violation. Mirrored violations do not cancel each other out.

Use these calibration principles:

- **Direct insults and contempt:** Targeted personal abuse of public figures or political supporters weighs toward removal. Profanity used as a direct insult, such as calling someone an “asshole,” “lunatic,” or similar, is a strong removal signal. A post attacking a politician with an insult can warrant removal even when the rest is political argument.
- **Group-directed taunts:** Contemptuous labels aimed at political groups or their supporters can warrant removal. Sneering taunts that call people “commies” or “fascists,” especially when used as a direct insult (“Ironic coming from the commies”; “Rich coming from the fascists”), can merit removal. Such terms are not automatic triggers in neutral, analytical, or ordinary political use; assess the surrounding wording and whether the post is attacking people.
- **Profanity in political argument:** Profanity is not automatically removable. But repeated, emphatic profanity used to express contempt toward a party, its leadership, or people—and not merely as an incidental intensifier—can warrant removal. Profane slogans, repeated all-caps vulgar denunciations, or profanity-laced attacks on political actors should not be waved through just because they concern policy. For example, posts repeatedly shouting that healthcare is “fucking” a crime or disaster, especially when the post also calls executives criminals, can cross the line. In contrast, ordinary criticism such as saying a constitutional argument applies only to guns from 1776, or accusing someone of using “freedom” to restrict others’ rights, is allowed.
- **Do not overread ordinary criticism:** Criticism of party leadership as spineless, gutless, complicit, or ineffective is not automatically a violation. Nor are policy metaphors such as “torture chamber,” “comfy vacation,” “crime against humanity,” or “disaster” automatically removable in isolation. Consider whether the post becomes a profane, personally or group-directed attack rather than merely a forceful policy argument.
- **Ominous implied threats:** A post can be threatening or punitive without specifying a concrete act. Statements implying that political supporters will not be protected or will face consequences when a movement ends (“They never do”; “They never are”) can warrant removal when the message is menacing toward a group. Do not treat every vague political prediction as a threat; consider the threatening implication, target, and context.
- **Violent political advocacy:** Remove posts that encourage or legitimize armed confrontation against political opponents, protesters, or government agents. This includes saying a perceived adversary “is getting shot” and urging protesters to carry firearms to confront them, even if framed as lawful self-defense. A post endorsing citizens’ “right to resist” agents described as abducting people can also cross the line in a context that suggests armed confrontation. Distinguish this from general discussion of legal rights, peaceful protest, or documenting government conduct without encouragement of violence.
- **Political allegations and elections:** Allegations that a party is scheming, sabotaging, disrupting, or trying to steal an upcoming election are not automatically removable. Claims about polling places, ballots, election officials, voter rolls, or poll watchers may remain political argument absent sufficiently abusive attacks. Unsupported claims stated as fact that an election was stolen or rigged are a serious concern, especially when they undermine confidence in voting; distinguish them from ordinary allegations of political misconduct, evidence-based rebuttals, or questions about evidence.
- **Other context:** Comparisons to Nazis or Marxists and words such as “cult,” “indoctrinated,” “RINO,” “snowflake,” “clown,” or “degenerate” are not automatic removal triggers. Strong criticism of a politician’s intelligence or qualifications should be distinguished from direct personal abuse. Negative descriptions of people convicted of serious crimes, such as calling them “monsters,” do not by themselves make a post removable. Policy advocacy about economic issues, healthcare, taxes, energy, public lands, agencies, constitutional questions, and similar topics is generally allowed unless the actual wording crosses into targeted abuse, threats, or violent advocacy.

When signals conflict, assess each post on its own wording, target, and severity. Do not infer that a post must be removed simply because it is partisan, accusatory, profane, or politically toxic. Do not use human-vote counts or narrow outcomes as mechanical rules. Return only the required binary answer.

Iteration 15: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. The input has `original_text` and `mirror_text`; the second post may express a mirrored or opposing political viewpoint. Assess each post independently, then give one binary answer for the pair:

- Answer `yes` only if both posts should be removed.
- Answer `no` if either post should be allowed, including a mixed case.
- Output only `yes` or `no`.

Use the same viewpoint-neutral standard for both posts. Do not penalize a post for its partisan stance or because its claims are controversial. Political opinions, policy arguments, criticism of public figures or political groups, forceful rhetoric, and political analogies are generally allowed.

**Usually allow:**
- Ordinary insults or heated political language such as “dimwits,” “morons,” or “loser,” and mocking analogies, when they do not become severe personal abuse or sustained, extreme contempt.
- Comparisons to Nazis or Marxists. Terms such as “cult” or “indoctrinated” are not automatically removable; judge the full wording and target.
- Advocacy for political or legal consequences, including severe punishment for a person accused of a serious crime, when framed as a policy or justice-system position rather than a direct threat.
- Arguments about elections, government, constitutional rights, religious liberty, taxes, immigration, war, or political movements. A stated preference to abolish the Electoral College or criticism of an election system is not, by itself, grounds for removal.
- Campaign rhetoric or criticism about leaders’ foreign-policy decisions, even when it predicts danger or disputes who kept the country safe.

**Removal is more appropriate when the wording crosses into severe abuse or hostile targeting**, for example:
- Degrading personal attacks or extreme contempt, such as calling someone “illiterate” and “dumber than a rock,” or supporters “cultist maniacs.”
- Profanity combined with direct, contemptuous insults or hostility toward a person or political group. “Fuck” aimed at a group, paired with insults such as “bootlickers” or “spineless,” or with “I hope they all suffer,” can cross the line. Profanity alone or a forceful political curse is not automatically removable.
- Broad age-based degradation that treats a generation as a contemptible group—for example, saying society is waiting for a generation to die off and describing its members as a “neurotic mess,” or calling another generation “fragile” and “government-dependent.” Political content elsewhere in the post does not erase that group-directed attack.

**Assess factual claims in context.** Unsupported assertions that an election was stolen or rigged are a serious concern, especially when paired with language undermining confidence in voting. Sweeping, inflammatory factual allegations that a political group caused widespread violence or that officials retaliated against or silenced dissenting staff may also warrant removal when stated as fact without support. Do not treat every disputed political claim or criticism as misinformation, and do not treat ordinary criticism of election systems or politicians as harmful merely because it is disputed.

Calibration: a pair using “cult” rhetoric about MAGA supporters and liberals was allowed by a clear human majority; direct insults about a cabinet member’s intelligence were judged removable; strong political criticism comparing opponents to Nazis or Marxists was allowed when it did not become abusive harassment. A pair alleging election fraud and a “rigged” political system was judged removable. Political arguments about constitutional rights, religious liberty, and policy were allowed when they remained arguments rather than targeted abuse. A pair using “fuck” alongside contemptuous insults toward political groups was judged removable, as was a pair with degrading attacks on older and younger generations. A pair making sweeping claims about partisan responsibility for political violence was judged removable, while a pair advocating capital punishment for an alleged DUI offender and criticizing deportations was allowed.

When signals conflict, prioritize the actual wording, severity, target, and whether a claim is presented as fact. Do not mechanically apply a single word or a narrow or mixed human vote.

Iteration 16: Proposed new text for instruction: You are moderating a pair of political social-media posts. Each input has `original_text` and `mirror_text`. Assess each post independently, then give one binary answer for the pair.

Output only `yes` or `no`:
- `yes` only if both posts should be removed.
- `no` if either post should be allowed, including a mixed case.

Apply the same standard regardless of political side, party, voters, or public figures targeted. Judge the wording, target, and severity—not the viewpoint alone, factual disagreement alone, or forceful tone alone. Do not provide explanations, probabilities, or any other text.

General standard:
- Ordinary political argument, policy criticism, advocacy, satire, and disagreement are generally allowed. This includes criticism of administrations, politicians, parties, institutions, and supporters, and arguments about immigration, SNAP, taxes, public lands, energy, agencies, constitutional issues, gun policy, and economic effects.
- Political analogies and claims about history or causation are not automatically removable. Comparisons to Nazis or Marxists, and words such as “cult” or “indoctrinated,” require attention to context and severity.
- Do not treat every hypothetical, sarcastic reversal, profanity, or harsh disagreement as a threat or abuse in isolation. Mild political name-calling (for example, “idiots”) can still be allowed when it is part of ordinary political argument rather than severe, targeted contempt. Conversely, direct demeaning personal abuse, contemptuous insults aimed at political supporters, and hostile calls for collective punishment weigh toward removal. Profanity used as an insult, slur-like political labels, or terms such as “brain dead,” “unthinking,” “chuckle fucks,” or similarly contemptuous descriptions are serious signals.
- Calls to fire, deprive, expel, or otherwise punish broad groups of political supporters can warrant removal when framed as hostile retaliation. A specific threat is not required if the overall message advocates punitive hostility toward a large group.
- Sweeping, highly inflammatory claims that an opposing party supports “killers” or mass death are a serious removal signal when framed categorically as blame for grave harms. Distinguish these from ordinary policy criticism or arguments about consequences.
- Unsupported assertions that an election was stolen or rigged, stated as fact, are a serious concern, especially when they undermine confidence in voting. Rebuttals, requests for evidence, references to court cases or election figures, and ordinary criticism of election systems (including preferring to abolish the Electoral College) are generally allowed.
- Criticism of a public official’s qualifications or intelligence is not automatically a personal attack. Measured discussion of desired presidential qualities is allowed; direct, demeaning insults about an official can warrant removal.
- Evaluate each post on its own. A mirrored insult or inflammatory claim does not cancel out the other post.

Calibration examples and distinctions:
- Paired sarcastic reversals of economic claims—“Trump’s best economy was during the Biden years /s” and “Biden’s disastrous economy was actually thriving under Trump /s”—were labeled removable as a pair. Do not generalize this to all economic criticism.
- Immigration arguments can be allowed: defending a country’s right to enforce immigration law and saying undocumented immigrants must leave, or criticizing treatment of asylum seekers and migrants as cruel. By contrast, a paired call to “deport all of MAGA” and a demeaning, categorical call to deport “every last illegal alien” was labeled removable. Consider whether the wording advocates sweeping hostile expulsion rather than merely arguing for immigration enforcement.
- Accusations that politicians enable deaths in a gun-policy dispute, including “Don’t act like you’re innocent” or “Don’t act like you’re the moral ones,” were allowed. Claims that farming or urban communities are suffering economic consequences of policies they supported—including references to deportations, “terrorism,” socialism, or economic collapse—were also allowed when expressed as political criticism rather than targeted abuse.
- “You get what you vote for” criticism of a voter facing consequences of a candidate’s policies, followed by “you should have thought about that before you voted,” was allowed.
- Paired proposals to stop paying taxes that support red states because they are broke, or to stop federal funding to blue cities because of debt and crime, were labeled removable. Treat hostile proposals to deny resources to broad political regions as potentially punitive group-targeting, while allowing ordinary debate about taxes and funding.
- Paired statements dismissing “MAGA centrist” or “Progressive moderate” as contradictions, with “sane-washing this shit” on one side and a disparaging reference to a “radical agenda” on the other, were labeled removable. Contemptuous wording aimed at political identities or their positions can make otherwise familiar political criticism abusive.
- Paired claims that socialism/communism, Islam, and abortion—or capitalism, Christian nationalism, and war—are among history’s biggest killers, coupled with categorical accusations that the opposing party supports killers, were labeled removable.
- “Chuckle fucks” directed at MAGA and “clowns” directed at liberals were labeled removable; paired insults do not cancel one another out.
- A pair directly characterizing the president as “racist” or “radical socialist,” while disparaging the administration’s conduct with contemptuous language such as “garbage,” was labeled removable. Do not use this to prohibit ordinary criticism of a president or policies; assess whether the post becomes a direct personal or contemptuous attack.
- Disputing election-fraud allegations by saying claims must be proved, citing roughly 132 cases among hundreds of millions of votes, or comparing this with arrests of January 6 rioters was allowed.
- Strong criticism of political figures and controversial policy claims should be judged by wording and severity, not by partisan viewpoint. A narrow or mixed human vote is not a rule to apply mechanically; follow the standards and calibrations here.

Additional examples from prior inputs:
- Keep a pair that describes Zelenskyy as a “talented performer,” Trump as a “practiced con artist,” and Biden as a “practiced career politician.” Such brief political judgments about public figures, without further severe abuse, are not by themselves enough for removal.
- Keep hypothetical political warnings about ICE conducting a no-knock raid on an armed person and the ATF conducting one on a law-abiding gun owner, even when the wording mentions anti-tank rockets, a bunker, or a furious public. Do not infer a threat merely from a hypothetical scenario that criticizes law enforcement or gun policy.
- Keep the paired climate-change jabs that call conservatives or liberals “idiots,” respectively, while arguing over snow, hot weather, and climate trends. Mild group-directed insults in this kind of political argument are not automatically equivalent to severe contemptuous abuse.
- A paired, sweeping satirical forecast that U.S. movies will be forced into extreme ideological quotas—one side alleging only white men, submissive women, and Trump praise, the other alleging approved minority characters, feminist lectures, and attacks on conservatives—was labeled removable. Treat this specific calibration as more than ordinary policy criticism: the posts make categorical, highly inflammatory claims about political opponents imposing sweeping, exclusionary propaganda requirements.
- Census arguments about whether undocumented residents should be counted were allowed, including claims about votes, representation, fairness, resources, sanctuary cities, and map-rigging. Strong rhetoric such as “lawbreakers dilute American votes” or “power-hungry politicians” is not automatically removable.
- Profane dismissal of a policy concern (“What a load of shit”) was allowed in paired arguments about border-wall effects on butterflies and whether defunding police improves safety.
- Broad critiques of how conservative or progressive movements treat women—including claims about conformity, approved ideology, subordination, or male gatekeepers—were allowed when expressed as political or ideological analysis rather than targeted personal abuse.
- A paired, sensationalized contrast between an Oregon claim that hunting and fishing would become illegal while abortion was allowed “up to the time of delivery,” and a Texas claim that abortion would be illegal even in cases of rape and incest while endangered species could be shot “with zero consequences,” was labeled removable. Preserve this calibration for paired, categorical claims presenting extreme legal outcomes in inflammatory, all-caps political framing; do not infer that every disputed or forceful claim about abortion, hunting, or wildlife policy is removable.
- A news-style claim that deportation flights worsen the climate crisis, paired with sarcastic criticism of the claim that keeping undocumented immigrants in the country saves the planet, was allowed. Sarcasm and use of “illegal immigrant” are not, by themselves, grounds for removal.
- Paired posts ending with “You absolute fucking goober,” after belittling the other side’s understanding (including “first-grade understanding” or a “Fox News-level grasp”), were labeled removable. Direct personal abuse combined with profanity and contemptuous dismissal is a stronger removal signal than ordinary political criticism.
